class Book:
    """
    A class to represent a Book.

    Attributes:
        title (str): The title of the book.
        author (str): The author of the book.
        year_published (int): The year the book was published.
    """

    def __init__(self, title: str, author: str, year_published: int):
        """
        Constructs all the necessary attributes for the book object.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param year_published: The year the book was published.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.year_published = year_published

    def get_title(self) -> str:
        """
        Gets the book title.

        :return: The title of the book.
        """
        return self.title

    def set_title(self, title: str) -> None:
        """
        Sets the book title.

        :param title: The new title of the book.
        """
        self.title = title

    def get_author(self) -> str:
        """
        Gets the book author.

        :return: The author of the book.
        """
        return self.author

    def set_author(self, author: str) -> None:
        """
        Sets the book author.

        :param author: The new author of the book.
        """
        self.author = author

    def get_year_published(self) -> int:
        """
        Gets the year the book was published.

        :return: The year the book was published.
        """
        return self.year_published

    def set_year_published(self, year_published: int) -> None:
        """
        Sets the year the book was published.

        :param year_published: The new year the book was published.
        """
        self.year_published = year_published
```<#sep#>Implement a class `Book` with the following attributes:
- `title`: A string representing the title of the book.
- `author`: A string representing the author of the book.
- `published_year`: An integer representing the year the book was published.
- `genre`: A string representing the genre of the book.
- `isbn`: A string representing the ISBN of the book.

The class should include the following methods:
- `get_age()`: Returns the age of the book in years from the current date.
- `is_classic()`: Returns `True` if the book is a classic (published more than 50 years ago), otherwise returns `False`.

Additionally, implement a function `book_from_dict` that takes a dictionary with book metadata and returns an instance of the `Book` class.

**Sample Input:**
```python
book_dict = {
    'title': '1984',
    'author': 'George Orwell',
    'published_year': 1949,
    'genre': 'Dystopian',
    'isbn': '9780451524935'
}
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
book = book_from_dict(book_dict)
print(book.get_age())  # Output will vary depending on the current year
print(book.is_classic())  # True
```

```python
from datetime import datetime

class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, published_year, genre, isbn):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param published_year: The year the book was published.
        :param genre: The genre of the book.
        :param isbn: The ISBN of the book.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.published_year = published_year
        self.genre = genre
        self.isbn = isbn

    def get_age(self):
        """
        Calculates the age of the book in years from the current date.

        :return: The age of the book in years.
        """
        current_year = datetime.now().year
        return current_year - self.published_year

    def is_classic(self):
        """
        Determines if the book is a classic (published more than 50 years ago).

        :return: True if the book is a classic, False otherwise.
        """
        return self.get_age() > 50

def book_from_dict(book_dict):
    """
    Creates a Book instance from a dictionary containing book metadata.

    :param book_dict: A dictionary with book metadata.
    :return: A Book instance.
    """
    return Book(
        title=book_dict.get('title'),
        author=book_dict.get('author'),
        published_year=book_dict.get('published_year'),
        genre=book_dict.get('genre'),
        isbn=book_dict.get('isbn')
    )
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a class `OnlineStore` that manages an inventory of items and a shopping cart. The class should support the following operations:

1. `add_item(item_name, price)`: Adds an item with a specified price to the store. If the item already exists, update its price.
2. `remove_item(item_name)`: Removes an item from the store if it exists.
3. `list_items()`: Lists all items available in the store along with their prices.
4. `add_to_cart(item_name, quantity)`: Adds a specified quantity of an item to the shopping cart. If the item is not available in the store, do nothing.
5. `remove_from_cart(item_name, quantity)`: Removes a specified quantity of an item from the shopping cart. If the quantity to remove is greater than or equal to the quantity in the cart, remove the item entirely.
6. `cart_total()`: Returns the total cost of the items in the shopping cart.
7. `list_cart()`: Lists all items in the shopping cart along with their quantities and individual costs, and the total cost of the cart.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```
store = OnlineStore()
store.add_item("apple", 0.99)
store.add_item("banana", 0.59)
store.list_items()
store.add_to_cart("apple", 3)
store.add_to_cart("banana", 5)
store.list_cart()
store.remove_from_cart("apple", 1)
store.list_cart()
print(f"Final cart total: ${store.cart_total():.2f}")
```

**Expected Output:**
```
Item 'apple' added with price $0.99.
Item 'banana' added with price $0.59.
Items available in the store:
- apple: $0.99
- banana: $0.59
Added 3 of 'apple' to the cart.
Added 5 of 'banana' to the cart.
Items in your shopping cart:
- apple: 3 x $0.99 = $2.97
- banana: 5 x $0.59 = $2.95
Total cost: $5.92
Removed 1 of 'apple' from the cart.
Items in your shopping cart:
- apple: 2 x $0.99 = $1.98
- banana: 5 x $0.59 = $2.95
Total cost: $4.93
Final cart total: $4.93
```

```python
class OnlineStore:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize the store with empty item and cart dictionaries."""
        self.items = {}  # Dictionary to store items and their prices
        self.cart = {}   # Dictionary to store items in the shopping cart and their quantities

    def add_item(self, item_name, price):
        """Add an item with its price to the store. Update price if item already exists."""
        if item_name in self.items:
            print(f"Item '{item_name}' already exists. Updating the price.")
        self.items[item_name] = price
        print(f"Item '{item_name}' added with price ${price:.2f}.")

    def remove_item(self, item_name):
        """Remove an item from the store if it exists."""
        if item_name in self.items:
            del self.items[item_name]
            print(f"Item '{item_name}' removed.")
        else:
            print(f"Item '{item_name}' not found in the store.")

    def list_items(self):
        """List all items available in the store."""
        if not self.items:
            print("No items in the store.")
        else:
            print("Items available in the store:")
            for item, price in self.items.items():
                print(f"- {item}: ${price:.2f}")

    def add_to_cart(self, item_name, quantity=1):
        """Add a quantity of an item to the shopping cart if the item is available in the store."""
        if item_name not in self.items:
            print(f"Item '{item_name}' not available in the store.")
            return

        if item_name in self.cart:
            self.cart[item_name] += quantity
        else:
            self.cart[item_name] = quantity

        print(f"Added {quantity} of '{item_name}' to the cart.")

    def remove_from_cart(self, item_name, quantity=1):
        """Remove a quantity of an item from the shopping cart."""
        if item_name not in self.cart:
            print(f"Item '{item_name}' not found in the cart.")
            return

        if self.cart[item_name] <= quantity:
            del self.cart[item_name]
            print(f"Removed all of '{item_name}' from the cart.")
        else:
            self.cart[item_name] -= quantity
            print(f"Removed {quantity} of '{item_name}' from the cart.")

    def cart_total(self):
        """Calculate and return the total cost of the items in the shopping cart."""
        total = sum(self.items[item] * qty for item, qty in self.cart.items())
        return total

    def list_cart(self):
        """List all items in the shopping cart along with their quantities and individual costs, and the total cost."""
        if not self.cart:
            print("Your shopping cart is empty.")
        else:
            print("Items in your shopping cart:")
            for item, quantity in self.cart.items():
                print(f"- {item}: {quantity} x ${self.items[item]:.2f} = ${self.items[item] * quantity:.2f}")
            print(f"Total cost: ${self.cart_total():.2f}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class that simulates basic banking operations. The class should support the following functionalities:
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance. The deposit amount must be positive.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance. The withdrawal amount must be positive and should not exceed the current balance.
- **Check Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.

Your implementation should handle invalid operations gracefully by printing appropriate error messages.

**Input:**
- The first line contains an integer `n` (1 ≤ n ≤ 100), the number of operations.
- The next `n` lines describe the operations. Each operation is either:
  - `deposit X` where `X` is the amount to deposit.
  - `withdraw X` where `X` is the amount to withdraw.
  - `check_balance` to check the current balance.

**Output:**
- For each operation, print the result as specified in the method descriptions.

**Sample Input:**
```
5
deposit 100
withdraw 30
check_balance
withdraw 150
check_balance
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Deposited: 100. New Balance: 100
Withdrew: 30. Remaining Balance: 70
Current Balance: 70
Insufficient funds for withdrawal.
Current Balance: 70
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, initial_balance=0):
        self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Adds the specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
        Prints an error message if the amount is not positive.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            print(f"Deposited: {amount}. New Balance: {self.balance}")
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Subtracts the specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and does not exceed the current balance.
        Prints an error message if the amount is not positive or if there are insufficient funds.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if self.balance >= amount:
                self.balance -= amount
                print(f"Withdrew: {amount}. Remaining Balance: {self.balance}")
            else:
                print("Insufficient funds for withdrawal.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Prints the current balance of the account.
        """
        print(f"Current Balance: {self.balance}")

# Example usage:
# account = BankAccount(100)
# account.deposit(50)
# account.withdraw(30)
# account.check_balance()
# account.withdraw(150)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `BankAccount` class in Python. The class should support the following operations:

- **Initialization**: Create a bank account with an initial balance. If the initial balance is negative, set the balance to 0 and print an error message.
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance. If the deposit amount is negative, print an error message.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance. If the withdrawal amount is negative or exceeds the current balance, print an error message.
- **Get Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.

Your implementation should handle invalid transactions gracefully by printing appropriate error messages.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Input: `BankAccount(-100)`
  - Output: `Error: Initial balance cannot be negative.`
- Input: `account = BankAccount(100); account.deposit(-50)`
  - Output: `Error: Deposit amount cannot be negative.`
- Input: `account = BankAccount(100); account.withdraw(150)`
  - Output: `Error: Insufficient funds for this withdrawal.`
- Input: `account = BankAccount(100); account.deposit(50); account.withdraw(30)`
  - Output: `Deposited 50. New balance is 150.
            Withdrew 30. New balance is 120.`

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, initial_balance=0):
        """
        Initialize the bank account with an initial balance.
        If the initial balance is negative, set it to 0 and print an error message.
        """
        if initial_balance < 0:
            print("Error: Initial balance cannot be negative.")
            self.balance = 0
        else:
            self.balance = initial_balance

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount to the account balance.
        If the deposit amount is negative, print an error message.
        """
        if amount < 0:
            print("Error: Deposit amount cannot be negative.")
        else:
            self.balance += amount
            print(f"Deposited {amount}. New balance is {self.balance}.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account balance.
        If the withdrawal amount is negative or exceeds the current balance, print an error message.
        """
        if amount < 0:
            print("Error: Withdrawal amount cannot be negative.")
        elif amount > self.balance:
            print("Error: Insufficient funds for this withdrawal.")
        else:
            self.balance -= amount
            print(f"Withdrew {amount}. New balance is {self.balance}.")

    def get_balance(self):
        """
        Return the current balance of the account.
        """
        return self.balance
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `LinkedList` class in Python. The class should support the following operations:

1. `add_at_beginning(data)`: Adds a new node with the specified data at the beginning of the list.
2. `add_at_end(data)`: Adds a new node with the specified data at the end of the list.
3. `remove_from_beginning()`: Removes the node from the beginning of the list and returns its data. Raises an `IndexError` if the list is empty.
4. `remove_from_end()`: Removes the node from the end of the list and returns its data. Raises an `IndexError` if the list is empty.
5. `is_present(data)`: Checks if a node with the specified data is present in the list and returns `True` or `False`.
6. `print_list()`: Prints all the elements in the list in order.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```
ll = LinkedList()
ll.add_at_end(10)
ll.add_at_end(20)
ll.add_at_beginning(5)
ll.print_list()  # Output: [5, 10, 20]
ll.remove_from_beginning()
ll.print_list()  # Output: [10, 20]
ll.remove_from_end()
ll.print_list()  # Output: [10]
print(ll.is_present(10))  # Output: True
print(ll.is_present(20))  # Output: False
```

```python
class Node:
    def __init__(self, data):
        self.data = data
        self.next = None

class LinkedList:
    def __init__(self):
        self.head = None
        self.tail = None

    def add_at_beginning(self, data):
        """Adds a new node with the specified data at the beginning of the list."""
        new_node = Node(data)
        if self.head is None:
            self.head = self.tail = new_node
        else:
            new_node.next = self.head
            self.head = new_node

    def add_at_end(self, data):
        """Adds a new node with the specified data at the end of the list."""
        new_node = Node(data)
        if self.tail is None:
            self.head = self.tail = new_node
        else:
            self.tail.next = new_node
            self.tail = new_node

    def remove_from_beginning(self):
        """Removes the node from the beginning of the list and returns its data. Raises an IndexError if the list is empty."""
        if self.head is None:
            raise IndexError("Remove from an empty list")
        data = self.head.data
        self.head = self.head.next
        if self.head is None:
            self.tail = None
        return data

    def remove_from_end(self):
        """Removes the node from the end of the list and returns its data. Raises an IndexError if the list is empty."""
        if self.tail is None:
            raise IndexError("Remove from an empty list")
        data = self.tail.data
        if self.head == self.tail:
            self.head = self.tail = None
        else:
            current = self.head
            while current.next != self.tail:
                current = current.next
            current.next = None
            self.tail = current
        return data

    def is_present(self, data):
        """Checks if a node with the specified data is present in the list and returns True or False."""
        current = self.head
        while current:
            if current.data == data:
                return True
            current = current.next
        return False

    def print_list(self):
        """Prints all the elements in the list in order."""
        elements = []
        current = self.head
        while current:
            elements.append(current.data)
            current = current.next
        print(elements)

class Book:

        """

        self.author = author
        self.ISBN = ISBN
        self.published_year = published_year
        self.genre = genre
        self.num_pages = num_pages

    def get_book_info(self):
        """
        Returns a string with the book's title, author, and ISBN.
        """
        return f"Title: {self.title}, Author: {self.author}, ISBN: {self.ISBN}"

    def get_published_year(self):
        """
        Returns the published year of the book.
        """
        return self.published_year

    def get_genre(self):
        """
        Returns the genre of the book.
        """
        return self.genre

    def get_num_pages(self):
        """
        Returns the number of pages in the book.
        """
        return self.num_pages

    def update_genre(self, new_genre):
        """
        Updates the genre of the book to new_genre.
        """
        self.genre = new_genre

    def update_num_pages(self, new_num_pages):
        """
        Updates the number of pages in the book to new_num_pages.
        """
        self.num_pages = new_num_pages
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `TemperatureConverter` class in Python that supports temperature conversions between Celsius, Fahrenheit, and Kelvin. Your class should include the following methods:

- `celsius_to_fahrenheit(celsius)`: Converts a temperature from Celsius to Fahrenheit.
- `fahrenheit_to_celsius(fahrenheit)`: Converts a temperature from Fahrenheit to Celsius.
- `celsius_to_kelvin(celsius)`: Converts a temperature from Celsius to Kelvin.
- `kelvin_to_celsius(kelvin)`: Converts a temperature from Kelvin to Celsius.
- `fahrenheit_to_kelvin(fahrenheit)`: Converts a temperature from Fahrenheit to Kelvin.
- `kelvin_to_fahrenheit(kelvin)`: Converts a temperature from Kelvin to Fahrenheit.

**Sample Input/Output:**

- `celsius_to_fahrenheit(0)` should return `32.0`
- `fahrenheit_to_celsius(32)` should return `0.0`
- `celsius_to_kelvin(0)` should return `273.15`
- `kelvin_to_celsius(273.15)` should return `0.0`
- `fahrenheit_to_kelvin(32)` should return `273.15`
- `kelvin_to_fahrenheit(273.15)` should return `32.0`

```python
class TemperatureConverter:
    def celsius_to_fahrenheit(self, celsius):
        """
        Converts a temperature from Celsius to Fahrenheit.
        
        :param celsius: Temperature in Celsius
        :return: Temperature in Fahrenheit
        """
        return (celsius * 9/5) + 32

    def fahrenheit_to_celsius(self, fahrenheit):
        """
        Converts a temperature from Fahrenheit to Celsius.
        
        :param fahrenheit: Temperature in Fahrenheit
        :return: Temperature in Celsius
        """
        return (fahrenheit - 32) * 5/9

    def celsius_to_kelvin(self, celsius):
        """
        Converts a temperature from Celsius to Kelvin.
        
        :param celsius: Temperature in Celsius
        :return: Temperature in Kelvin
        """
        return celsius + 273.15

    def kelvin_to_celsius(self, kelvin):
        """
        Converts a temperature from Kelvin to Celsius.
        
        :param kelvin: Temperature in Kelvin
        :return: Temperature in Celsius
        """
        return kelvin - 273.15

    def fahrenheit_to_kelvin(self, fahrenheit):
        """
        Converts a temperature from Fahrenheit to Kelvin.
        
        :param fahrenheit: Temperature in Fahrenheit
        :return: Temperature in Kelvin
        """
        celsius = self.fahrenheit_to_celsius(fahrenheit)
        return self.celsius_to_kelvin(celsius)

    def kelvin_to_fahrenheit(self, kelvin):
        """
        Converts a temperature from Kelvin to Fahrenheit.
        
        :param kelvin: Temperature in Kelvin
        :return: Temperature in Fahrenheit
        """
        celsius = self.kelvin_to_celsius(kelvin)
        return self.celsius_to_fahrenheit(celsius)
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple game board for a turn-based strategy game. The board is a 10x10 grid. Implement a class `GameBoard` with the following functionalities:

- `place_unit(unit_id, x, y, player)`: Place a unit with a unique identifier `unit_id` at position `(x, y)` on the board. The unit belongs to `player`. Raise a `ValueError` if the position is invalid or already occupied.
- `move_unit(unit_id, x, y)`: Move the unit with `unit_id` to position `(x, y)`. Raise a `ValueError` if the unit does not exist, it is not the unit owner's turn, the position is invalid, or the destination is occupied by another unit of the same player.
- `check_collision(x, y)`: Return `True` if there is a unit at position `(x, y)`, otherwise return `False`.
- `end_turn()`: End the current player's turn and switch to the next player.

Players take turns moving their units, starting with player 0.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```
board = GameBoard()
board.place_unit('unit1', 0, 0, 0)
board.place_unit('unit2', 0, 1, 1)
board.move_unit('unit1', 1, 0)
print(board.check_collision(1, 0))  # Output: True
board.end_turn()
print(board.turn)  # Output: 1
```

```python
class GameBoard:
    def __init__(self):
        self.board = [[None for _ in range(10)] for _ in range(10)]
        self.units = {}
        self.turn = 0  # Player 0 starts the game

    def _is_valid_position(self, x, y):
        return 0 <= x < 10 and 0 <= y < 10

    def place_unit(self, unit_id, x, y, player):
        """
        Place a unit with a unique identifier `unit_id` at position `(x, y)` on the board.
        The unit belongs to `player`. Raise a `ValueError` if the position is invalid or already occupied.
        """
        if not self._is_valid_position(x, y):
            raise ValueError("Invalid position")
        if self.board[x][y] is not None:
            raise ValueError("Position already occupied")
        self.board[x][y] = unit_id
        self.units[unit_id] = {"position": (x, y), "player": player}

    def move_unit(self, unit_id, x, y):
        """
        Move the unit with `unit_id` to position `(x, y)`. Raise a `ValueError` if the unit does not exist,
        it is not the unit owner's turn, the position is invalid, or the destination is occupied by another
        unit of the same player.
        """
        if unit_id not in self.units:
            raise ValueError("Unit does not exist")
        if self.units[unit_id]['player'] != self.turn:
            raise ValueError("Not the unit owner's turn")
        if not self._is_valid_position(x, y):
            raise ValueError("Invalid position")
        if self.board[x][y] is not None and self.units[self.board[x][y]]['player'] == self.turn:
            raise ValueError("Destination is occupied by another unit of the same player")
        cur_x, cur_y = self.units[unit_id]['position']
        self.board[cur_x][cur_y] = None
        self.board[x][y] = unit_id
        self.units[unit_id]['position'] = (x, y)

    def check_collision(self, x, y):
        """
        Return `True` if there is a unit at position `(x, y)`, otherwise return `False`.
        """
        return self.board[x][y] is not None

    def end_turn(self):
        """
        End the current player's turn and switch to the next player.
        """
        self.turn = self.turn ^ 1  # Simple toggle between players 0 and 1
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple e-commerce shopping cart system. Your system should support the following operations:

1. **Add Product**: Add a product to the cart. If the product already exists, increase its quantity.
2. **Remove Product**: Remove a product from the cart. If the product does not exist, raise a `ValueError`.
3. **Update Quantity**: Update the quantity of a product in the cart. If the product does not exist, raise a `ValueError`.
4. **Total Price**: Calculate the total price of all products in the cart.
5. **Display Cart**: Display the contents of the cart, including the product name, price, quantity, and total price for each product, followed by the total price of the cart.

Products are represented as dictionaries with the following keys:
- `product_name`: The name of the product (string).
- `price`: The price of a single unit of the product (float).
- `quantity`: The quantity of the product in the cart (integer).

Implement a class `ShoppingCart` that supports the above operations. Your implementation should handle invalid operations gracefully by raising appropriate exceptions.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```python
cart = ShoppingCart()
cart.add_product("Apple", 0.5, 10)
cart.add_product("Banana", 0.3, 5)
cart.display_cart()
# Expected Output:
# Shopping Cart:
# --------------
# Apple - $0.5 * 10 = $5.0
# Banana - $0.3 * 5 = $1.5
# --------------
# Total Price: $ 6.5
# --------------

cart.update_quantity("Apple", 15)
cart.remove_product("Banana")
cart.display_cart()
# Expected Output:
# Shopping Cart:
# --------------
# Apple - $0.5 * 15 = $7.5
# --------------
# Total Price: $ 7.5
# --------------

try:
    cart.remove_product("Orange")
except ValueError as e:
    print(e)
# Expected Output:
# Product 'Orange' is not in the cart.

try:
    cart.update_quantity("Orange", 1)
except ValueError as e:
    print(e)
# Expected Output:
# Product 'Orange' not found in the cart.
```

```python
class ShoppingCart:
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty shopping cart."""
        self.products = []

    def add_product(self, product_name, price, quantity):
        """
        Add a product to the cart. If the product already exists, increase its quantity.
        
        :param product_name: Name of the product (str).
        :param price: Price of a single unit of the product (float).
        :param quantity: Quantity of the product to add (int).
        """
        for product in self.products:
            if product['product_name'] == product_name:
                product['quantity'] += quantity
                return
        self.products.append({'product_name': product_name, 'price': price, 'quantity': quantity})

    def remove_product(self, product_name):
        """
        Remove a product from the cart. Raise ValueError if the product does not exist.
        
        :param product_name: Name of the product to remove (str).
        :raises ValueError: If the product is not in the cart.
        """
        for product in self.products:
            if product['product_name'] == product_name:
                self.products.remove(product)
                return
        raise ValueError(f"Product '{product_name}' is not in the cart.")

    def update_quantity(self, product_name, new_quantity):
        """
        Update the quantity of a product in the cart. Raise ValueError if the product does not exist.
        
        :param product_name: Name of the product to update (str).
        :param new_quantity: New quantity of the product (int).
        :raises ValueError: If the product is not in the cart.
        """
        for product in self.products:
            if product['product_name'] == product_name:
                product['quantity'] = new_quantity
                return
        raise ValueError(f"Product '{product_name}' not found in the cart.")

    def total_price(self):
        """
        Calculate the total price of all products in the cart.
        
        :return: Total price (float).
        """
        return sum(product['price'] * product['quantity'] for product in self.products)

    def display_cart(self):
        """
        Display the contents of the cart, including product name, price, quantity, and total price for each product,
        followed by the total price of the cart.
        """
        print("Shopping Cart:")
        print("--------------")
        for product in self.products:
            print(f"{product['product_name']} - ${product['price']} * {product['quantity']} = ${product['price'] * product['quantity']}")
        print("--------------\nTotal Price: $", self.total_price(), "\n")
        print("--------------")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Car` class that simulates basic car functionalities. The class should have the following attributes and methods:
- `make`: A string representing the car's manufacturer.
- `model`: A string representing the car's model.
- `year`: An integer representing the car's manufacturing year.
- `fuel_level`: A float representing the current fuel level in liters, initialized to `0`.
- `fill_tank(amount)`: A method that increases the `fuel_level` by the specified `amount` in liters.
- `drive(distance)`: A method that decreases the `fuel_level` by `distance / 10` liters. If the `fuel_level` is insufficient for the trip, print "Not enough fuel to drive the distance." and set `fuel_level` to `0`. Otherwise, print "Driven {distance} km. Remaining fuel: {remaining_fuel:.2f} liters."

Your task is to create an instance of the `Car` class, fill its tank with a given amount of fuel, and simulate driving a specified distance. If the car runs out of fuel during the trip, it should be indicated in the output.

**Sample Input:**
```
Toyota Corolla 2021
50
100
500
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Driven 100 km. Remaining fuel: 40.00 liters.
Not enough fuel to drive the distance.

```python
class Car:
    def __init__(self, make, model, year):
        """
        Initializes a new Car instance with the given make, model, and year.
        The fuel level is initialized to 0 liters.
        """
        self.make = make
        self.model = model
        self.year = year
        self.fuel_level = 0

    def fill_tank(self, amount):
        """
        Increases the fuel level by the specified amount in liters.
        
        :param amount: The amount of fuel to add to the tank.
        """
        self.fuel_level += amount

    def drive(self, distance):
        """
        Simulates driving the car for a specified distance.
        Decreases the fuel level by distance / 10 liters.
        Prints a message indicating the distance driven and remaining fuel.
        If there is not enough fuel, prints a message and sets fuel level to 0.
        
        :param distance: The distance to drive in kilometers.
        """
        fuel_needed = distance / 10
        if self.fuel_level >= fuel_needed:
            self.fuel_level -= fuel_needed
            print(f"Driven {distance} km. Remaining fuel: {self.fuel_level:.2f} liters.")
        else:
            print("Not enough fuel to drive the distance.")
            self.fuel_level = 0

# Create an instance of Car

# Simulate a car trip
my_car.fill_tank(50)  # Fill the tank with 50 liters of fuel
my_car.drive(100)     # Drive 100 km
my_car.drive(500)     # Try to drive 500 km with the remaining fuel

class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, isbn, publication_year):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance with the given title, author, isbn, and publication_year.
        
        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param isbn: The ISBN of the book.
        :param publication_year: The year the book was published.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.publication_year = publication_year

    def is_classic(self):
        """
        Determines if the book is a classic, defined as being published before 1950.
        
        :return: True if the book is a classic, False otherwise.
        """
        return self.publication_year < 1950

    Book("To Kill a Mockingbird", "Harper Lee", "9780060935467", 1960),
    Book("Pride and Prejudice", "Jane Austen", "9781503290617", 1813),
    Book("The Great Gatsby", "F. Scott Fitzgerald", "9780743273565", 1925)
]

# Checking if each book is a classic and printing the result
for book in books:
    print(f"'{book.title}' by {book.author} is a classic: {book.is_classic()}")
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Matrix` class in Python that supports basic matrix operations such as addition and multiplication. The class should be able to handle matrices of real numbers and should be initialized with a 2D list. Implement the following methods:

- `__init__(self, data)`: Initializes the matrix with the given 2D list `data`.
- `__add__(self, other)`: Implements matrix addition. Raises a `ValueError` if the matrices do not have the same dimensions.
- `__mul__(self, other)`: Implements matrix multiplication. Raises a `ValueError` if the number of columns in the first matrix is not equal to the number of rows in the second matrix.
- `__str__(self)`: Returns a string representation of the matrix for easy printing.

**Input:**
- The input will be provided through the `Matrix` class constructor and the `__add__` and `__mul__` methods.

**Output:**
- The `__str__` method should return a string representation of the matrix, with each row on a new line and elements separated by spaces.

**Sample Input:**
```python
m1 = Matrix([[1, 2, 3], [4, 5, 6]])
m2 = Matrix([[7, 8], [9, 10], [11, 12]])
m3 = m1 + m1
m4 = m1 * m2
```

**Sample Output:**
```
2 4 6
8 10 12
58 64
139 154
```

```python
class Matrix:
    def __init__(self, data):
        """
        Initializes the matrix with the given 2D list `data`.
        
        :param data: A 2D list of real numbers representing the matrix.
        """
        self.data = data
        self.rows = len(data)
        self.cols = len(data[0]) if self.rows > 0 else 0

    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Implements matrix addition.
        
        :param other: Another Matrix instance to be added to this matrix.
        :return: A new Matrix instance representing the sum of the two matrices.
        :raises ValueError: If the matrices do not have the same dimensions.
        """
        if not isinstance(other, Matrix) or self.rows != other.rows or self.cols != other.cols:
            raise ValueError("Matrices must be of the same dimensions to add.")
        result = [[self.data[i][j] + other.data[i][j] for j in range(self.cols)] for i in range(self.rows)]
        return Matrix(result)

    def __mul__(self, other):
        """
        Implements matrix multiplication.
        
        :param other: Another Matrix instance to be multiplied with this matrix.
        :return: A new Matrix instance representing the product of the two matrices.
        :raises ValueError: If the number of columns in the first matrix is not equal to the number of rows in the second matrix.
        """
        if not isinstance(other, Matrix) or self.cols != other.rows:
            raise ValueError("Number of columns in first matrix must be equal to number of rows in second matrix for multiplication.")
        result = [[sum(self.data[i][k] * other.data[k][j] for k in range(self.cols)) for j in range(other.cols)] for i in range(self.rows)]
        return Matrix(result)

    def __str__(self):
        """
        Returns a string representation of the matrix for easy printing.
        
        :return: A string representation of the matrix.
        """
        return '\n'.join([' '.join(map(str, row)) for row in self.data])
```<#sep#>You are given a string representing an arithmetic expression consisting of non-negative integers, `+`, `-`, `*`, `/` operators, and parentheses `()`. Your task is to evaluate the expression and return the result as an integer. The expression is guaranteed to be valid and contain no spaces.

**Sample Input:**
```
"3+(4*2)-1"
```

**Sample Output:**
```
10
```

```python
def evaluate_expression(expression: str) -> int:
    """
    Evaluates an arithmetic expression given as a string.
    
    :param expression: A string representing the arithmetic expression.
    :return: The result of the evaluated expression as an integer.
    """
    def calculate(operand_stack, operator_stack):
        while operator_stack and operand_stack:
            if operator_stack[-1] in precedence:
                right = operand_stack.pop()
                left = operand_stack.pop()
                op = operator_stack.pop()
                operand_stack.append(operations[op](left, right))
            else:
                break

    def precedence(op):
        return {'+': 1, '-': 1, '*': 2, '/': 2}.get(op, 0)

    def apply_operator(operators, operands):
        right = operands.pop()
        left = operands.pop()
        op = operators.pop()
        operands.append(operations[op](left, right))

    precedence_order = {'+': 1, '-': 1, '*': 2, '/': 2}
    operations = {'+': lambda a, b: a + b, '-': lambda a, b: a - b,
                  '*': lambda a, b: a * b, '/': lambda a, b: a // b}
    operand_stack = []
    operator_stack = []
    index = 0
    while index < len(expression):
        if expression[index].isdigit():
            start = index
            while index < len(expression) and expression[index].isdigit():
                index += 1
            operand_stack.append(int(expression[start:index]))
            index -= 1
        elif expression[index] in '+-*/':
            while (operator_stack and operator_stack[-1] != '(' and
                   precedence_order[operator_stack[-1]] >= precedence_order[expression[index]]):
                apply_operator(operator_stack, operand_stack)
            operator_stack.append(expression[index])
        elif expression[index] == '(':
            operator_stack.append(expression[index])
        elif expression[index] == ')':
            while operator_stack and operator_stack[-1] != '(':
                apply_operator(operator_stack, operand_stack)
            operator_stack.pop()
        index += 1
    while operator_stack:
        apply_operator(operator_stack, operand_stack)
    return operand_stack[0]

# Test
print(evaluate_expression("3+(4*2)-1"))  # Output: 10
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Car` class in Python. The class should encapsulate the following attributes: `make`, `model`, `year`, and `mileage`. The `make`, `model`, and `year` should be private attributes, while `mileage` should be a protected attribute. Implement the following functionalities:

1. A constructor that initializes the car's make, model, year, and optionally mileage (default to 0).
2. A method to update the mileage of the car. The mileage should only be allowed to increase, not decrease. If an attempt is made to set a lower mileage, raise a `ValueError`.
3. A method to display the car's information in the format: `Car(make='make', model='model', year=year, mileage=mileage)`.

Your implementation should include proper encapsulation and docstrings for the class and its methods.

**Sample Input:**
```python
car = Car("Toyota", "Corolla", 2020, 15000)
print(car.display_info())
car.update_mileage(20000)
print(car.display_info())
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Car(make='Toyota', model='Corolla', year=2020, mileage=15000)
Car(make='Toyota', model='Corolla', year=2020, mileage=20000)
```

```python
class Car:
    """
    A class to represent a car with attributes for make, model, year, and mileage.
    It provides methods to update the mileage and display the car's information.
    """

    def __init__(self, make, model, year, mileage=0):
        """
        Initializes the Car object with make, model, year, and optional mileage.
        Mileage defaults to 0 if not provided.

        :param make: str, the make of the car
        :param model: str, the model of the car
        :param year: int, the year the car was manufactured
        :param mileage: int, the mileage of the car, default is 0
        """
        self.__make = make
        self.__model = model
        self.__year = year
        self._mileage = mileage

    def update_mileage(self, mileage):
        """
        Updates the mileage of the car. Only allows increasing the mileage,
        not decreasing it.

        :param mileage: int, the new mileage to be set
        :raises ValueError: if the provided mileage is less than the current mileage
        """
        if mileage >= self._mileage:
            self._mileage = mileage
        else:
            raise ValueError("Mileage cannot be decreased.")

    def display_info(self):
        """
        Displays the car's information, including make, model, year, and mileage.

        :return: str, a formatted string containing the car's information
        """
        return f"Car(make='{self.__make}', model='{self.__model}', year={self.__year}, mileage={self._mileage})"
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a simple number guessing game. The game involves guessing a randomly generated integer between 1 and 100. The player has a maximum of 10 attempts to guess the correct number. After each guess, the program should provide feedback indicating whether the guess is too low, too high, or correct. If the player guesses the number correctly within the allowed attempts, they win; otherwise, they lose.

**Input:**
- The player will input their guess as an integer between 1 and 100.

**Output:**
- For each guess, output one of the following:
  - "Your guess is too low."
  - "Your guess is too high."
  - "Congratulations! You guessed the number X correctly in Y attempts. You win!"
- If the player exhausts all attempts without guessing correctly, output:
  - "Sorry, you have used all your attempts. The number was X. Better luck next time!"

**Sample Interaction:**
```
Welcome to the Number Guessing Game!
I'm thinking of a number between 1 and 100. You have 10 attempts to guess it.
Enter your guess: 50
Your guess is too low.
Attempts remaining: 9
Enter your guess: 75
Your guess is too high.
Attempts remaining: 8
Enter your guess: 62
Your guess is too low.
Attempts remaining: 7
Enter your guess: 68
Your guess is too high.
Attempts remaining: 6
Enter your guess: 65
Congratulations! You guessed the number 65 correctly in 5 attempts. You win!
```

```python
import random

def number_guessing_game():
    """
    Simulates a number guessing game where the player has to guess a randomly generated number between 1 and 100.
    The player has 10 attempts to guess the number. The program provides feedback after each guess and declares
    the result at the end of the game.
    """
    number_to_guess = random.randint(1, 100)
    attempts_remaining = 10

    print("Welcome to the Number Guessing Game!")
    print("I'm thinking of a number between 1 and 100. You have 10 attempts to guess it.")

    while attempts_remaining > 0:
        try:
            player_guess = int(input("Enter your guess: "))
        except ValueError:
            print("Invalid input. Please enter a number between 1 and 100.")
            continue
        
        if player_guess == number_to_guess:
            print(f"Congratulations! You guessed the number {number_to_guess} correctly in {10 - attempts_remaining + 1} attempts. You win!")
            break
        elif player_guess < number_to_guess:
            print("Your guess is too low.")
        elif player_guess > number_to_guess:
            print("Your guess is too high.")
        
        attempts_remaining -= 1
        print(f"Attempts remaining: {attempts_remaining}")

        if attempts_remaining == 0:
            print(f"Sorry, you have used all your attempts. The number was {number_to_guess}. Better luck next time!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    number_guessing_game()
```<#sep#>Implement a class `LinkedList` in Python that supports basic operations for a singly linked list. The class should include the following methods:

- `__init__(self)`: Initializes an empty linked list.
- `append(self, value)`: Adds a new node with the given value to the end of the list.
- `prepend(self, value)`: Adds a new node with the given value to the beginning of the list.
- `delete(self, value)`: Removes the first occurrence of a node with the given value from the list.
- `find(self, value)`: Returns the first node that contains the given value, or `None` if the value is not found.
- `__str__(self)`: Returns a string representation of the linked list, with node values separated by ' -> '.
- `__len__(self)`: Returns the number of nodes in the list.

The class should be well-documented with docstrings for each method.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```python
ll = LinkedList()
ll.append(1)
ll.append(2)
ll.prepend(0)
print(ll)  # Output: 0 -> 1 -> 2
print(len(ll))  # Output: 3
ll.delete(1)
print(ll)  # Output: 0 -> 2
print(len(ll))  # Output: 2
node = ll.find(2)
print(node.value if node else "Not found")  # Output: 2
node = ll.find(1)
print(node.value if node else "Not found")  # Output: Not found
```

```python
class Node:
    """A node in a singly linked list."""
    def __init__(self, value):
        """Initialize a node with the given value."""
        self.value = value
        self.next = None

class LinkedList:
    """A singly linked list."""
    def __init__(self):
        """Initialize an empty linked list."""
        self.head = None
        self.size = 0

    def append(self, value):
        """Add a new node with the given value to the end of the list."""
        new_node = Node(value)
        if self.head is None:
            self.head = new_node
        else:
            current = self.head
            while current.next:
                current = current.next
            current.next = new_node
        self.size += 1

    def prepend(self, value):
        """Add a new node with the given value to the beginning of the list."""
        new_node = Node(value)
        new_node.next = self.head
        self.head = new_node
        self.size += 1

    def delete(self, value):
        """Remove the first occurrence of a node with the given value from the list."""
        current = self.head
        previous = None
        while current:
            if current.value == value:
                if previous:
                    previous.next = current.next
                else:
                    self.head = current.next
                self.size -= 1
                return
            previous = current
            current = current.next

    def find(self, value):
        """Return the first node that contains the given value, or None if the value is not found."""
        current = self.head
        while current:
            if current.value == value:
                return current
            current = current.next
        return None

    def __str__(self):
        """Return a string representation of the linked list, with node values separated by ' -> '."""
        nodes = []
        current = self.head
        while current:
            nodes.append(str(current.value))
            current = current.next
        return ' -> '.join(nodes)

    def __len__(self):
        """Return the number of nodes in the list."""
        return self.size
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a Python class `FileProcessor` that handles file operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

1. **Initialization**: Accept a list of file paths.
2. **Reading Files**: Read the contents of all files and return a list of strings, where each string contains the contents of a file. If a file cannot be read, append `None` to the list and print an error message.
3. **Writing to File**: Write the provided content to the specified output file path. Print an error message if an error occurs during writing.
4. **Filtering Files**: Filter the list of files based on a keyword and return a list of file paths that contain the keyword in their content.

**Sample Input**:
- File paths: `["file1.txt", "file2.txt"]`
- Contents of `file1.txt`: `"Hello World"`
- Contents of `file2.txt`: `"Goodbye World"`
- Keyword for filtering: `"World"`

**Sample Output**:
- `read_files()` should return `["Hello World", "Goodbye World"]`
- `filter_files("World")` should return `["file1.txt", "file2.txt"]`

```python
class FileProcessor:
    def __init__(self, file_paths):
        """
        Initialize the FileProcessor with a list of file paths.

        :param file_paths: List of file paths to be processed.
        """
        self.file_paths = file_paths

    def read_files(self):
        """
        Read the contents of all files and return a list of strings.

        :return: List of file contents as strings, or None for files that could not be read.
        """
        contents = []
        for file_path in self.file_paths:
            try:
                with open(file_path, 'r') as file:
                    contents.append(file.read())
            except FileNotFoundError:
                print(f"Error: The file {file_path} was not found.")
                contents.append(None)
            except IOError:
                print(f"Error: An error occurred while reading the file {file_path}.")
                contents.append(None)
        return contents

    def write_to_file(self, output_file_path, content):
        """
        Write the provided content to the specified output file path.

        :param output_file_path: Path to the output file.
        :param content: Content to be written to the file.
        """
        try:
            with open(output_file_path, 'w') as file:
                file.write(content)
        except IOError:
            print(f"Error: An error occurred while writing to the file {output_file_path}.")

    def filter_files(self, keyword):
        """
        Filter the list of files based on a keyword and return a list of file paths.

        :param keyword: Keyword to search for in file contents.
        :return: List of file paths containing the keyword.
        """
        filtered_paths = []
        contents = self.read_files()
        for file_path, content in zip(self.file_paths, contents):
            if content is not None and keyword in content:
                filtered_paths.append(file_path)
        return filtered_paths
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Calculator` class in Python that supports basic arithmetic operations: addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division. The class should include methods for each operation. Each method should validate its inputs to ensure they are either integers or floats. If the inputs are invalid, the method should raise a `ValueError`. For the division method, if the divisor is zero, a `ZeroDivisionError` should be raised.

**Sample Input/Output:**
- Input: `calc = Calculator(); calc.add(10, 5)`
- Output: `15`
- Input: `calc = Calculator(); calc.divide(10, 0)`
- Output: Raises `ZeroDivisionError` with message "Cannot divide by zero"
- Input: `calc = Calculator(); calc.multiply(3, 'a')`
- Output: Raises `ValueError` with message "Inputs must be integers or floats"

```python
class Calculator:
    def add(self, a, b):
        """
        Adds two numbers and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Sum of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        return a + b

    def subtract(self, a, b):
        """
        Subtracts the second number from the first and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Difference of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        return a - b

    def multiply(self, a, b):
        """
        Multiplies two numbers and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Product of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        return a * b

    def divide(self, a, b):
        """
        Divides the first number by the second and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Quotient of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        :raises ZeroDivisionError: If the second number is zero
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        if b == 0:
            raise ZeroDivisionError("Cannot divide by zero")
        return a / b
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class for a library management system. The class should encapsulate the following attributes: `title`, `author`, `isbn`, `publisher`, `year_published`, `language`, `page_count`, `available_copies`, and `genres`. The `genres` attribute should be a list of genres associated with the book.

Implement the `Book` class with the following methods:
- `is_available()`: Returns `True` if there is at least one available copy of the book, otherwise returns `False`.
- `check_out_book()`: Decreases the `available_copies` by one if the book is available. If the book is not available, print the message "Error: Book is not available for check out."

```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, isbn, publisher, year_published, language, page_count, available_copies, genres=None):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance with the given attributes.
        
        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param isbn: The ISBN of the book.
        :param publisher: The publisher of the book.
        :param year_published: The year the book was published.
        :param language: The language the book is written in.
        :param page_count: The number of pages in the book.
        :param available_copies: The number of available copies of the book.
        :param genres: A list of genres associated with the book.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.publisher = publisher
        self.year_published = year_published
        self.language = language
        self.page_count = page_count
        self.available_copies = available_copies
        self.genres = genres if genres is not None else []

    def is_available(self):
        """
        Checks if the book is available for check out.
        
        :return: True if the book is available, False otherwise.
        """
        return self.available_copies > 0

    def check_out_book(self):
        """
        Checks out a book if it is available, otherwise prints an error message.
        """
        if self.is_available():
            self.available_copies -= 1
        else:
            print("Error: Book is not available for check out.")

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Returns a book, increasing the number of available copies by one.
        """
        self.available_copies += 1

    def add_genre(self, genre):
        """
        Adds a new genre to the book's genres list if it is not already present.
        
        :param genre: The genre to be added.
        """
        if genre not in self.genres:
            self.genres.append(genre)
```<#sep#>Implement a class `BST` that represents a binary search tree (BST). The class should support the following operations:
- `insert(key)`: Inserts a new node with the given key into the BST.
- `delete(key)`: Deletes the node with the given key from the BST if it exists.
- `search(key)`: Returns the node with the given key if it exists in the BST, otherwise returns `None`.
- `inorder()`: Returns a list of keys in the BST in inorder traversal order.

**Input:**
- The first line contains an integer `n` (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^4), the number of operations.
- The next `n` lines each contain an operation of the form:
  - `insert x`: Insert the key `x` into the BST.
  - `delete x`: Delete the key `x` from the BST.
  - `search x`: Search for the key `x` in the BST. Print `1` if found, `0` otherwise.
  - `inorder`: Print the keys in the BST in inorder traversal order.

**Output:**
- For each `search` operation, print `1` if the key is found, `0` otherwise.
- For each `inorder` operation, print the keys in the BST in inorder traversal order.

**Sample Input:**
```
10
insert 50
insert 30
insert 20
insert 40
insert 70
insert 60
insert 80
search 40
inorder
delete 20
```

**Sample Output:**
```
1
20 30 40 50 60 70 80
```

```python
class TreeNode:
    def __init__(self, key):
        self.left = None
        self.right = None
        self.val = key

class BST:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = None

    def insert(self, key):
        """Inserts a new node with the given key into the BST."""
        if self.root is None:
            self.root = TreeNode(key)
        else:
            self._insert(self.root, key)

    def _insert(self, node, key):
        if key < node.val:
            if node.left is None:
                node.left = TreeNode(key)
            else:
                self._insert(node.left, key)
        else:
            if node.right is None:
                node.right = TreeNode(key)
            else:
                self._insert(node.right, key)

    def search(self, key):
        """Returns the node with the given key if it exists in the BST, otherwise returns None."""
        return self._search(self.root, key)

    def _search(self, node, key):
        if node is None or node.val == key:
            return node

        if key < node.val:
            return self._search(node.left, key)

        return self._search(node.right, key)

    def delete(self, key):
        """Deletes the node with the given key from the BST if it exists."""
        self.root = self._delete(self.root, key)

    def _delete(self, node, key):
        if node is None:
            return node

        if key < node.val:
            node.left = self._delete(node.left, key)
        elif key > node.val:
            node.right = self._delete(node.right, key)
        else:
            if node.left is None:
                return node.right
            elif node.right is None:
                return node.left

            node.val = self._min_value_node(node.right).val
            node.right = self._delete(node.right, node.val)

        return node

    def _min_value_node(self, node):
        current = node

        while current.left is not None:
            current = current.left

        return current
		
    def inorder(self):
        """Returns a list of keys in the BST in inorder traversal order."""
        return self._inorder(self.root)
		
    def _inorder(self, node):
        res = []
        if node:
            res = self._inorder(node.left)
            res.append(node.val)
            res = res + self._inorder(node.right)
        return res
```<#sep#>Implement a `BankAccount` class in Python that simulates basic bank account operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: Create a bank account with an account number and an optional initial balance (default is 0.0).
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
- **Get Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.
- **Display Transactions**: Print all transactions made on the account.

**Input/Output Specification**:
- The `deposit` and `withdraw` methods will be called with positive numerical values.
- The `get_balance` method will return a numerical value representing the current balance.
- The `display_transactions` method will print a list of transactions.

**Sample Usage**:
```python

print(account.get_balance())

**Expected Output**:

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_number, initial_balance=0.0):
        """
        Initialize a new bank account with an account number and an optional initial balance.
        
        :param account_number: A string representing the account number.
        :param initial_balance: A float representing the initial balance (default is 0.0).
        """
        self.account_number = account_number
        self.balance = initial_balance
        self.transactions = []

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
        
        :param amount: A float representing the amount to deposit.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            self.transactions.append(amount)
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
        
        :param amount: A float representing the amount to withdraw.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if self.balance >= amount:
                self.balance -= amount
                self.transactions.append(-amount)
            else:
                print("Insufficient funds.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def get_balance(self):
        """
        Return the current balance of the account.
        
        :return: A float representing the current balance.
        """
        return self.balance

    def display_transactions(self):
        """
        Print all transactions made on the account.
        """
        print("Transactions:", self.transactions)
```<#sep#>You are given two English texts and an integer `n`. Your task is to implement a function `get_common_words(text1: str, text2: str, n: int) -> List[str]` that identifies the `n` most common words shared between the two texts, excluding a predefined list of common stop words. The function should return these words in descending order of their combined frequency across both texts.

**Input:**
- `text1`: A string representing the first text.
- `text2`: A string representing the second text.
- `n`: An integer representing the number of common words to return.

**Output:**
- A list of `n` strings, each representing a common word between the two texts, sorted in descending order of their combined frequency.

**Constraints:**
- The input texts are in English.
- The function should ignore common stop words such as "the", "and", "is", etc.
- Assume that the input texts do not contain any special characters other than punctuation marks.

**Sample Input:**
```python
text1 = "The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog."
text2 = "The lazy dog is not amused by the quick brown fox."
n = 2
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
['the', 'quick']
```

```python
from collections import Counter
from typing import List

def get_common_words(text1: str, text2: str, n: int) -> List[str]:
    """
    Returns the n most common words shared between two given texts, excluding common stop words.
    
    :param text1: A string representing the first text.
    :param text2: A string representing the second text.
    :param n: An integer representing the number of common words to return.
    :return: A list of n strings, each representing a common word between the two texts, sorted in descending order of their combined frequency.
    """
    # Define a set of common stop words.
    stop_words = set([
        "the", "and", "is", "in", "it", "to", "of", "a", "that", "i", "for", "on", "with", "as", "you", 
        "he", "she", "at", "by", "this", "from", "or", "which", "one", "an", "be", "have", "has", "had", 
        "but", "not", "so", "if", "then", "when", "where", "why", "how", "all", "any", "both", "each", 
        "few", "more", "most", "other", "some", "such", "no", "nor", "not", "only", "own", "same", "than",
        "too", "very", "s", "t", "can", "will", "just", "don", "should", "now"
    ])
    
    def clean_text(text: str) -> List[str]:
        # Lowercase the text and split it into words.
        words = text.lower().split()
        # Remove punctuation and stop words.
        words = [word.strip(".,!?;:'\"()[]{}") for word in words if word.lower() not in stop_words]
        return words
    
    # Clean both texts.
    words1 = clean_text(text1)
    words2 = clean_text(text2)
    
    # Count the frequency of each word in both texts.
    freq1 = Counter(words1)
    freq2 = Counter(words2)
    
    # Find the common words in both texts.
    common_words = freq1.keys() & freq2.keys()
    
    # Sort the common words by frequency in descending order.
    common_words_by_freq = sorted(common_words, key=lambda w: (freq1[w] + freq2[w]), reverse=True)
    
    # Return the top n words.
    return common_words_by_freq[:n]
```<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class to manage a library's inventory. The class should support the following operations:

- **Initialization**: Create a book with the following attributes:
  - `title`: The title of the book.
  - `author`: The author of the book.
  - `isbn`: The International Standard Book Number.
  - `publication_year`: The year the book was published.
  - `pages`: The number of pages in the book.
  - `language`: The language in which the book is written.
  - `genre`: The genre of the book.
  - `available_copies`: The number of copies available in the library (initially 0).
  - `borrowed_copies`: The number of copies currently borrowed from the library (initially 0).

- **Methods**:
  - `add_copy(num_copies)`: Add a specified number of copies to the library. Raise a `ValueError` if `num_copies` is negative.
  - `borrow_copy()`: Borrow a copy from the library. Raise a `ValueError` if no copies are available.
  - `return_copy()`: Return a copy to the library. Raise a `ValueError` if no copies are currently borrowed.
  - `display_details()`: Print the book's details in the following format:
    ```
    Title: <title>
    Author: <author>
    ISBN: <isbn>
    Publication Year: <publication_year>
    Pages: <pages>
    Language: <language>
    Genre: <genre>
    Available Copies: <available_copies>
    Borrowed Copies: <borrowed_copies>
    ```

**Sample Input/Output**:
```
Input:
book = Book("1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935", 1949, 328, "English", "Dystopian")
book.add_copy(5)
book.borrow_copy()
book.display_details()
book.return_copy()

Output:

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, isbn, publication_year, pages, language, genre):
        """
        Initialize a new book with the given attributes.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.publication_year = publication_year
        self.pages = pages
        self.language = language
        self.genre = genre
        self.available_copies = 0
        self.borrowed_copies = 0

    def add_copy(self, num_copies=1):
        """
        Add a specified number of copies to the library.
        Raise a ValueError if num_copies is negative.
        """
        if num_copies < 0:
            raise ValueError("Number of copies to add must be non-negative.")
        self.available_copies += num_copies

    def borrow_copy(self):
        """
        Borrow a copy from the library.
        Raise a ValueError if no copies are available.
        """
        if self.available_copies <= 0:
            raise ValueError("No copies are available to borrow.")
        self.available_copies -= 1
        self.borrowed_copies += 1

    def return_copy(self):
        """
        Return a copy to the library.
        Raise a ValueError if no copies are currently borrowed.
        """
        if self.borrowed_copies <= 0:
            raise ValueError("No copies are currently borrowed to return.")
        self.available_copies += 1
        self.borrowed_copies -= 1

    def display_details(self):
        """
        Print the book's details in a user-friendly format.
        """
        details = (
            f"Title: {self.title}\n"
            f"Author: {self.author}\n"
            f"ISBN: {self.isbn}\n"
            f"Publication Year: {self.publication_year}\n"
            f"Pages: {self.pages}\n"
            f"Language: {self.language}\n"
            f"Genre: {self.genre}\n"
            f"Available Copies: {self.available_copies}\n"
            f"Borrowed Copies: {self.borrowed_copies}"
        )
        print(details)
```<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `sales_data.csv` containing sales transactions. Each transaction is represented by a row with the following columns: `transaction_id`, `product_category`, and `transaction_amount`. Your task is to write a Python program that reads this CSV file and generates a report with the following information for each product category:
- Total sales
- Average sales per transaction
- Number of transactions

The CSV file is guaranteed to be well-formed and contain no missing or invalid data.

**Sample Input:**
```
transaction_id,product_category,transaction_amount
1,Electronics,299.99
2,Clothing,49.99
3,Electronics,199.99
4,Clothing,99.99
5,Books,15.99
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Category: Electronics
  Total Sales: $499.98
  Average Transaction Value: $249.99
  Number of Transactions: 2
----------------------------------------
Category: Clothing
  Total Sales: $149.98
  Average Transaction Value: $74.99
  Number of Transactions: 2
----------------------------------------
Category: Books
  Total Sales: $15.99
  Average Transaction Value: $15.99
  Number of Transactions: 1
----------------------------------------
```

```python
import csv
from collections import defaultdict

def analyze_sales_data(csv_file):
    """
    Analyzes sales data from a CSV file and generates a report with total sales,
    average sales per transaction, and number of transactions for each product category.

    :param csv_file: Path to the CSV file containing sales data.
    :return: A list of dictionaries, each containing the category, total sales,
             average sales per transaction, and number of transactions.
    """
    with open(csv_file, mode='r', newline='') as file:
        csv_reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        category_total_sales = defaultdict(float)
        category_transaction_count = defaultdict(int)
    
        for row in csv_reader:
            category = row['product_category']
            amount = float(row['transaction_amount'])
            category_total_sales[category] += amount
            category_transaction_count[category] += 1

    report = []
    for category in category_total_sales:
        total_sales = category_total_sales[category]
        transaction_count = category_transaction_count[category]
        average_sales = total_sales / transaction_count if transaction_count > 0 else 0
        report.append({
            "category": category,
            "total_sales": total_sales,
            "average_sales": average_sales,
            "transaction_count": transaction_count
        })

    return report

if __name__ == "__main__":
    sales_report = analyze_sales_data("sales_data.csv")
    for entry in sales_report:
        print(f"Category: {entry['category']}")
        print(f"  Total Sales: ${entry['total_sales']:.2f}")
        print(f"  Average Transaction Value: ${entry['average_sales']:.2f}")
        print(f"  Number of Transactions: {entry['transaction_count']}")
        print("-" * 40)
